Codex art prompts

Updated 2026-10-19 (v13). The author decided on 19 October that the painted WIDTH no longer fails a picture: Art scales a returned picture UNIFORMLY at packing (one factor on both axes, no shear) until its base fits the guide's held width, and a uniform scale distorts nothing. The width is a measurement now. The camera (kept strict: a picture off the game's camera is repainted and never corrected, issue #536), the plan's foot ratio, the height as a proportion of the base width, the light, the same building as r0, and one new test, the base inside the footprint after scaling, decide. All 51 pictures that Codex painted on 19 October were re-tested on that rule (47 of them had been refused for width only; numbers per attempt in handoff/codex-review-1019b.json). One passes: depot a0 r3, attempt 3 (assets/source/buildings-v2/raw/depot-a0-r3-attempt-3.png on art/325-buildings-v2, sha256 487bdc41..., scaled by 0.806 at packing). It is ACCEPTED and is not painted again, so C34a is off the page. Every other picture fails the camera (feet too steep or too flat), most also the plan or the footprint, so the cards below are the same jobs with the width gate turned into a measurement, the line that told the painter to paint smaller taken out, and the re-test numbers of each failed view; every card starts with what is accepted (nothing, apart from the depot). Earlier fixes stay: C21a and C23a (Step 0 restores their farm and quarry pictures from a backup snapshot) and R7c-1 (its highlight test is a report). Three lanes, each its own Codex window: the buildings lane (the narrow depot and the farm halt first, then the quarry, the coaling stage, the Fuel Pump and the windmill), the rotations lane (the turned views of the old base-v1 pictures on the branch art/535-rotations, the ones that block gameplay first; each job writes its own measuring script and rebases before it pushes), and the people lane. Every prompt reads the numbers of the review-2 guides (PR #537, not merged yet: each prompt tells Codex how to read them out of that branch without committing them to its art branch); at most three attempts a view. The house biome prompt, C33b, waits for the house rotations to be accepted.

C35a

Narrow depot a0, never painted: r0 to r3 on the 2 x 1 guides (4 pictures)

Buildings lane. Branch art/325-buildings-v2 (exists, head cd7919ec or later). Issue #325. ACCEPTED: none (re-tested on 19 October after the author's width decision; numbers per attempt in handoff/codex-review-1019b.json). NEEDED: all four views (r0 to r3), never painted. The depot a0 pictures it attaches for the family look are on the branch now (586865bf). The queue lists depot_narrow a0 to a5 as pending; this paints the Steam age. A small single-track engine shed, one portal per end wall, matching the depot's a0 in materials and colours. Guides guides/t1x2-r0.png to r3 (1024 x 1024, 8 px to a game px) are in the tree. The base is held at 707 px of the guide (865 of 1254), feet 481 : 225 at r0 and r2, 225 : 481 at r1 and r3. r0 first, then r1, r3, r2; the depot's a0 r0 is attached for the family look only.

Paint the narrow depot (family depot_narrow, Steam age a0) for the isometric railway game in this
repository, for issue #325: all four views, r0 first, then r1, r3 and r2. It has never been painted.
It is a small single-track engine shed for narrow-gauge trains, 2 x 1 tiles, which trains run
through: a narrow hall with one portal in each end wall. It must match the depot family's a0 in
style: the same materials, colours and details, smaller and narrower.

Work on branch art/325-buildings-v2, which exists (head cd7919ec or later; art/325-fuel-pump is the
Fuel Pump's, not this one): git fetch origin art/325-buildings-v2, git switch art/325-buildings-v2,
git pull --ff-only. The pictures this job needs must be in your tree:
assets/source/buildings-v2/depot/depot-a0-r0.png (the finished depot, for the family's look), and
the guides assets/source/buildings-v2/guides/t1x2-r0.png, t1x2-r1.png, t1x2-r2.png and t1x2-r3.png.
If one is missing, stop and say so. Touch nothing outside assets/source/buildings-v2/. Commit there,
never stash pictures, push the branch. Never push to main or develop. Commit subjects read "Art:
<outcome>". Keep every picture your image tool returns, byte for byte, as raw/<id>-attempt-<k>.png
(k from 1 for each id, or going on from the number raw/returns.json already holds), and add {id,
attempt, file, returned, spans, top, height, kept} to raw/returns.json for each call. Ask for 1024 x
1024, transparent background, and record the size it returns.

WHY AGAIN. The queue lists depot_narrow a0 to a5, 24 pictures, all pending; this job paints the
Steam age. The family has no base picture in the game (families.json: base null), so there is no old
art to turn: r0 is painted on the guide from the family's own text and the depot's a0 picture.
Footprint 2 x 1 tiles at r0 (1 x 2 at r1 and r3), canvas 1024 x 1024, 8 px to a game px, anchor
(512, 800); the guides are in your tree. The family text for a0: a timber plank shed on a cream
stone footing with a slate roof and one round-arched portal per end; match the depot a0 (brick and

materials and colours, so the two sheds belong together.

THE GUIDE. They are in your tree: assets/source/buildings-v2/guides/t1x2-r0.png to t1x2-r3.png, 1024
x 1024 (check they exist and have that size; the queue names them for depot_narrow). Each is a grey

mark (one, 0.44 tile wide and 24 px high) on the end wall that has it, and the crew door mark (0.16
tile wide, 18 px high) on the front long wall where the camera sees it. No review-2 guide is needed.

THE PAINTER'S SPREAD (revised 19 October). The painter fills the grey PLINTH rather than the block that stands on it, so its building comes out wider than the block. The author decided on 19 October that this no longer refuses a picture (THE SIZE, below). Still, in the picture prompt of EVERY attempt, the first included, add this sentence after the printed prompt and the view's Correction: "The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge." It is one sentence, not a second Correction.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The
base's foot is 706.6 px wide from the left corner to the right corner in the guide (865 px in a 1254
px picture, 1.2246 times as many) in every view: that is the held width. Art scales a returned
picture UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is that wide. A
uniform scale distorts nothing, so the painted width no longer refuses a picture, and you do not try
to hit it: paint the building at the size that comes naturally, and never resize it yourself. Record
the base's width and the scale k = held width / measured width. The camera, the plan (the foot
ratio), the height (a proportion), the light and the same building decide; and the footprint after
scaling (below), because parts that stand off the plinth are still wrong once the picture is scaled.

PUT THE VIEWS BACK. Run node tools/building-queue.mjs status first and note the state of this
family. Nothing is to be put back: all four are pending. Do not run redo. Then run status again and
check that depot_narrow-a0-r0 to r3 are pending (and no other family has changed). Do not run
"next"; use show and take with each id, in this order: depot_narrow-a0-r0, depot_narrow-a0-r1,
depot_narrow-a0-r3, depot_narrow-a0-r2.

EACH VIEW. show <id> lists the references and prints the prompt and its "edit this" line, which
names the queue's guide. Paint as an edit of that guide
(assets/source/buildings-v2/guides/t1x2-r<v>.png), the first picture attached, and then the
references show lists, in its order; After them attach
assets/source/buildings-v2/depot/depot-a0-r0.png, the depot's a0 front view, as the family's look
only: take its materials, colours and details, not its size and not its two-portal layout (this shed
has one portal per end). ask for the size it prints, transparent background. Take it with take <id>
--from <file>, look at .look/<id>.png, measure (below), then set <id> generated --note
"<measurements>". Paste the printed prompt whole, then the view's Correction below, on EVERY
attempt, the first included. Nothing else goes in the picture: no ground, plinth, shadow, text,
border, glow or extra object; real RGBA, nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0, nothing as strong
as selection amber #E8AA48.

For every view, add this paragraph after the printed prompt, on every attempt (it is not a
Correction: it fixes what the shed is; r0 is painted first, from the guide and the depot's a0 r0,
and a Correction goes under it only after a failed attempt): The shed, carried round all four views:
a narrow single-track hall of timber planks and brick on a cream stone footing, a slate gable roof
with a smoke vent along the ridge, ONE round-arched portal in each end wall with evergreen timber
doors folded open, a small crew door and two small windows in the front long wall. Narrower and
smaller than the depot: the portal is 0.44 tile wide and 24 game px high.

For r1 and r3 (each from the guide and r0; the tool's printed prompt says the rest) and r2 (from the
guide, r0, r1 and r3): the same shed turned, not mirrored; in r1 and r2 the front faces away and no
crew door or front window shows on a visible wall; the portal is on the end wall the guide marks.

Where the printed prompt has the portal paragraph, keep it as it stands; if the picture's portals
fail the tool's check, ask in the tool's own words, as they stand: "Inside each doorway paint the
hall's inner wall, in shadow. Only the floor is left out: below that inner wall the ground inside
the doorway, up to about a third of the doorway's height, and the ground before it stay unpainted
and transparent, so that the game's rails show there." Do not sharpen them.

MEASURE BEFORE YOU RECORD (after take, before set), in this order:

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. On a crop, read the outer edge of the base's left corner and of its right corner (the
  foot of the walls, outline included; roofs, chimneys and anything above the ground do not count)
  and their span in the returned picture's px, and k = the held width (707 px of the guide, 865 of a 1254 px
  picture) / that span. Record both; the picture goes on to the fit whatever its span;

- node tools/building-fit.mjs <id>. Its base corners w, s and e give the lower-left foot s.x - w.x
  and the lower-right foot e.x - s.x. A picture passes when:

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view:
  2.136 in r0 and r2 (1.82 to 2.46, the long side lower left), 0.468 in r1 and r3 (0.40 to 0.54, the
  long side lower right);

- camera: at most 2 degrees off the game's (the tool's check, which set runs; node
  tools/building-check.mjs <file> prints it). Measure the plan first: if the feet are not in the plan's
  ratio, do not set it, paint it again. A picture that fails the camera is repainted, never
  stretched (issue #536);

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, piers and the
  like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is made again with those parts brought
  inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the walls' height at the near corner s, foot to the eaves (the block's 34 game px, 272 px
  of the guide), over e.x - w.x within 15 per cent of the guide's 0.385; r1 to r3 against the kept
  r0;

- highlights (reported, NOT a pass rule: tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them): count opaque pixels with all three channels at or above #ECE4D0, and within 12 (RGB
  distance) of #E8AA48, on your picture and on r0 (its fitted picture); neither count may exceed
  r0's (Art caps the rest at packing);

- by eye: one shed of one size in all four views, the same planks, brick, stone footing, slate and
  vent as the depot's a0 (look at them side by side); the portal only on the end wall the guide
  marks (the tool's check names the wall), with the hall's inner wall in shadow inside it and the
  ground left open for the rails (the narrow depot's track is narrow and has room to either side);
  no crew door or window of the front on a hidden front; no plinth painted; the lower-left wall
  lighter than the lower-right wall.

A picture refused for its camera alone, that passes the other tests, is handled by the tool
(GUIDE.md section 7: next hands it out to paint again from its own earlier self). A picture that
fails the plan, the height or the footprint is made again from the printed prompt: keep the view's
Correction and add ONE more paragraph that begins "Correction:" and names the number it missed, as
"your last picture's lower-left foot was 0.62 of the lower-right one where 2.14 is wanted"; it replaces the added one
from the attempt before. Never attach a picture that failed to paint again from, and never
stretch, squeeze, shear or bend a picture: the angles come from the camera, in the painting. At most
three attempts a view, 12 calls in all. If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, run set <id>
rejected --note "<the test it failed and its number>" (the tool sets the file aside), and say so in
your message; never accept it quietly.

CLOSE. Only the Steam age a0 is painted here: do not start a1. node tools/building-check.mjs
--family depot_narrow; node tools/building-sheets.mjs --family depot_narrow, then look at
review/depot_narrow.png: all four views should be one shed of one size, and beside the depot one
family. Commit "Art: narrow depot a0, r0 to r3 on the t1x2 guides", push the branch and stop. In
your message give the review sheet's path and, for each attempt: the view, its base span (px of the returned picture), k and the held span, the largest overshoot outside the plinth, the feet and ratio, the height number, the camera error, the
highlight counts, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which pictures passed every test.

MEASURE BEFORE YOU RECORD (after take, before set), in this order:

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. On a crop, read the outer edge of the base's left corner and of its right corner (the
  foot of the walls, outline included; roofs, chimneys and anything above the ground do not count)
  and their span in the returned picture's px, and k = the held width (416 px of the guide, 509 of a 1254 px
  picture) / that span. Record both; the picture goes on to the fit whatever its span;

  and the lower-right foot e.x - s.x. A picture passes when:

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of 0.795 (0.68 to 0.91) in r1 and
  r3, the long side lower right;

- camera: at most 2 degrees off the game's (the tool's check, which set runs; node
  tools/building-check.mjs <file> prints it). Measure the plan first: if the feet are not in the plan's
  ratio, do not set it, paint it again. A picture that fails the camera is repainted, never
  stretched (issue #536);

  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, piers and the
  like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is made again with those parts brought
  inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): (the near corner's row - box.top) over e.x - w.x, within 15 per cent of the same number
  for r0's fitted picture, both from node tools/building-fit.mjs;

- highlights (reported, NOT a pass rule: tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them): count opaque pixels with all three channels at or above #ECE4D0, and within 12 (RGB
  distance) of #E8AA48, on your picture and on r0 (its fitted picture); neither count may exceed
  r0's (Art caps the rest at packing);

- by eye: r3 turned, not mirrored (door and loading hatch on the lower-right wall); no door or hatch
  on r1; the same barn, silo and roof as r0; no plinth painted; the foot of both visible walls plain
  and straight; the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall.

WHY AGAIN. C4d's nine attempts all failed. The 62 game px block-out cured the low views only
partly, because the guide was a square block on a thin plinth, and the painter spread the stage
wider than the plinth: in the kept attempts of r1, r3 and r2 (9, 9 and 8) the six piers' feet
spanned 794, 737 and 725 px of the 1254 px picture, against 632 for the plinth: 26, 17 and 15 per
cent wider. That is measured on all nine of C4d's pictures: Codex paints the stage 15 to 26 per cent
wider than the plinth. The fit scales a picture by its feet's span, so a wider stage comes out
lower: the height shortfall, and with the same freedom came the wrong plan (r1's long side 36
per cent too long, r3's short side widened until the plan read 1.069, nearly square), the
camera drift, and r3's winch in the middle of the wall instead of at the near end. So the guide
is new: an OBLONG block on an oblong plinth of the footprint's width, with the six stone pier
stubs standing on it at the exact places of the stage's six piers, at the same 62 px block height.
The pier span is measured and recorded first; it no longer refuses a picture (THE SIZE, below).

UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its piers' span is that. A uniform
scale distorts nothing, so the painted span no longer refuses a picture, and you do not try to hit
it: paint the stage at the size that comes naturally, and never resize it yourself. Record the span

MEASURE BEFORE YOU RECORD (after take, before set), in this order:
- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. On a crop, read the leftmost pier's foot and the
  rightmost pier's foot (outer edges; chute and chain do not count) and their span in the returned
  picture's px, and k = 632 / that span (scaled by returned width / 1024 if the picture is another
  size). Record both; the picture goes on to the fit whatever its span;
- node tools/building-fit.mjs <id>. Its base corners w, s and e give the lower-left foot
  s.x - w.x and the lower-right foot e.x - s.x. A picture passes when:
- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s, (546.7, 944.6) in r1 and r3 (the plinth's corners there are w (256, 814.7), s (546.7, 960), e (768, 849.3)). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, piers and the
  like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is made again with those parts brought
  inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;
- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): (s.y - box.top) / (e.x - w.x), with the
  base corners s, e and w that node tools/building-fit.mjs prints for the picture as it was returned
  (the pier span e.x - w.x) and box.top its opaque box's top row, within 10% of the same number for
  r0's picture read the same way: 1.531 (s.y 1128, box.top 154, span 636 of 1254; r2 reads 1.484).
  That is 1.378 to 1.684. The older reading, (944.64 - box.top) / 450.56 of the fitted picture (r0
  1.464), is the same proportion only while the fit has not had to stretch the picture to the
  camera: do not use it on a picture whose camera is off. C4d's views were 1.062 to 1.372 by it;
- plan: lower-left foot / lower-right foot, between 1.16 and 1.57 (r0's 1.364 turned over), within
  15%; the long side lower left, as above;
- camera: at most 2 degrees off the game's (checkFile(file, 't1', rot) in tools/building-check.mjs
  and cameraOff in tools/building-fit.mjs, which set runs; node tools/building-check.mjs <file>
  prints it). Measure the plan and the height first: if the plan is wrong or the picture is low, do not set it, paint it again;
- highlights (reported, NOT a pass rule: tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them): count opaque pixels with all three channels at or above #ECE4D0, and within 12
  (RGB distance) of #E8AA48, on your picture and on r0 (its fitted picture); neither count may
  exceed r0's (Art caps the rest at packing);
- by eye: r3 turned, not mirrored (winch, chain and chute at the lower-right wall's near end,
  inside the guide's opening); no chute, winch or chain on a hidden wall; six piers, three along
  the long side; no plinth painted; the same bunker, coal, straps and legs as r0; lower-left wall
  lighter than lower-right.
A picture refused for its camera alone, that passes the other tests, is handled by the tool
(GUIDE.md section 7: `next` hands it out to paint again from its own earlier self; also attach
.fitted/fuel_stop-a0-r0.png). A picture that fails the plan, the height or the footprint is made again
from the printed prompt: keep every Correction paragraph of that view and add one that begins
"Correction:" and names the number it missed, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.66 and 0.57 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". Never attach a picture that failed to paint again from, and
never stretch, squeeze, shear or bend a picture: the angles come from the camera, in the painting.
At most three attempts a view, six calls in all. If none passes, keep the closest in raw/, run
set <id> rejected --note "<the test it failed and its number>" (the tool sets the file aside), and
say so in your message; never accept it quietly.

THE BUILDING (r0). A red-brick pump house on cream limestone quoins and base. Its front is the long
side: two bays, the door bay (forest-green plank door, lamp over it, a small slate-roofed porch
gable a little forward of the wall), a downpipe, a plain bay, and a big quoined pier at the end. The
short side and the back are plain brick between quoins: no door, no window, no porch, no lamp. On
the house a dark steel frame with X bracing, and on it a riveted forest-green steel tank with two
oat-cream bands, a railing, a manhole and a vent, as long as the long side and as deep as the house,
covering the whole roof. At the end of the front next to the left-hand side wall stands a tall
forest-green hose crane: ONE post with one diagonal brace and ONE jib about 0.43 tile long (the
width of the short side), the hose hanging from the jib's end. Pipes with copper bands run from the
tank to the crane. THE LIGHT never turns with the building. It comes from the upper left: tops
lightest, the lower-left wall mid (r0's front, luma 112), the lower-right wall darkest (r0's short
side, 67). Camera: orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, no perspective; both wall feet fall
one pixel for every two across.

STEP 0, before any call: measure r0's own feet. Run node
assets/source/fuel-2026-10-11/measure-plan.mjs on fuel_pump-a1-r0.png and read the house's outer
quoin corners by eye on a zoomed crop (the crane, the porch and the end pier can throw measureBase).
The guides are built on measureBase's reading of r0, 441 px for the front and 344 px for the side,
of 785 px in a 1254 px picture: a long side over short side of 1.28, not R8d's 2.4. Record both
readings in prompts.json. If your eye-read ratio of front over side is outside 1.1 to 1.5, stop and
say so, with the numbers, before painting anything: the guides would then be wrong and Art must hear
it.

THE PLAN, per view, in guide px (1024 x 1024 canvas, 8 px to a game px, anchor (512, 832); a picture
comes back 1254 px wide, 1.2246 times the guide: scale every number by returned width / 1024).

r1 (front faces the upper left, hidden): the base's feet w (307, 844.7), s (486.6, 934.5), e (717,
819.3); lower-left foot 179.6, lower-right foot 230.4, lower-left over lower-right 0.779 (the
house's long side over its short side: 1.283); the long side is lower right; block 44 game px.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The
grey plinth is the footprint, 516 px wide in the guide (632 px in a 1254 px wide picture), and the base
stands on it and fills 90 per cent of its long side (0.9 of 1 tiles). Art scales a returned picture
UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width, 412 px
of the guide (505 of 1254). A uniform scale distorts nothing, so the painted width no longer refuses
a picture, and you do not try to hit it: paint the house at the size that comes naturally, and never
resize it yourself. For the Fuel Pump, in every view, measure the base's width from the outer edge of
the house's left corner to the outer edge of its right corner at ground level, outline included (the
crane's post, the pier and the porch count only where they stand on the ground), and record it with
the scale k = held width / measured width. The camera, the plan, the height (a proportion of the
base width), the light, the crane and the same building decide; and the footprint after scaling
(below), because parts that stand off the plinth are still wrong once the picture is scaled.

(3), and (4) and (5) the kept new r1 and r3 (only if they passed; attach what passed). Never attach
more.

side is wide (about 92 px on the guide), not longer; the hose hangs from its end."

pier at each end, no door, no window, no porch, no lamp. The tank lies along the long lower-right

outside the base, rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow

MEASURE EVERY ATTEMPT, in this order (record-attempt.mjs writes prompts.json; its plan test still
holds R8d's 2.0 to 2.8: change it, in your own folder, to the window below, and say so):

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture, and
  k = 505 / that width (scaled by returned width / 1024 if the picture is another size). Record
  both; the picture goes on to the next tests whatever its width;

- plan: the house's long side over its short side (measure-plan.mjs; read the outer quoin corners by
  eye where the crane, pipe or pier throws it, and record both) within 15 per cent of 1.283: 1.09 to
  1.48, the long side on the side the plan names: lower right in r1 and r3, lower left in r2;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). The crane's post, the
  pier, the porch and the hose standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted
  with those parts brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the brick wall at the near corner, foot to the top of its quoins, over the base's width
  e.x - w.x: 0.39 to 0.47 (r0 0.43). Not lower than r0's;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50, at most 2 degrees by checkFile for t1 and the
  view's rotation (record-attempt.mjs runs it). A picture off by more is repainted, never
  straightened (issue #536); a picture within 3 degrees that passes the rest may be kept, and is
  recorded as such;

- light: light-check.mjs with a box on plain brick of the lower-left wall, then one of the
  lower-right wall, clear of quoins and the crane: the lower-right median at most 0.75 times the
  lower-left's, the lower-left between 100 and 150 and the lower-right between 60 and 85;

- crane: exactly one post and one jib, no second post and no beam over the tank; the post's top
  above the tank's top; the jib's reach 0.43 tile, about 92 px of the guide (75 to 110 px); the hose
  ending at the door's top in r3;

- look, on grass as in review/: set beside r0 it is the same building, the plain walls plain, no
  door, porch or lamp on a hidden front, the crane at the corner the paragraph says.

THE PROMPT, word for word, every attempt (then any Correction paragraphs below it):

"Edit this grey block-out into ONE finished stone tower windmill WITHOUT SAILS, for a pastoral
isometric railway game. Style: the attached style board; crisp clustered shading, broad readable
masses, three or four shades per material. Materials as the third and fourth pictures: cream
limestone courses with a few grey stones, a dark slate cone cap, an evergreen plank door with
iron straps. Light from the upper left: the cap's left side and the tower's upper left lightest,
the lower-left face mid-tone, the lower-right face darkest. Nothing brighter than a soft cream: no
pure-white highlights, nothing orange.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera and turn exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees
down. The tower is round and smooth, no facets: its foot is an ellipse half as tall as it is
wide, and every stone course curves like that foot (down in the middle of the face), not as a
straight line. Keep the tower's size, taper and place, and the cone cap's height: the cone's tip
stands where the block-out's does, with a plain tip, no finial, no ball. The dark door stays on
the LOWER-LEFT face where the block-out has it, at ground level on the tower's foot, as tall as the
block-out's door (about a quarter of the tower's foot width), an evergreen plank door with iron
straps and a plain stone frame; no step, no sack, nothing in front. One narrow window may sit
above it on the same face, on the same centre line. The windshaft: keep the block-out's stub, a
short round shaft of dark iron-banded timber coming out of the cap's lower-left slope and pointing
down and to the left along the lower-left face, two pixels across for each pixel down, and
ending in the block-out's dark iron hub plate, the same oval at the same place; the plate is bare,
with nothing on it.

Do not paint the grey slab: the tower stands straight on the ground, its foot plain and clean. No
sails, no stocks, no timber lattice, no square iron plate, no shadow, ground, grass, sacks, people,
text, border or glow. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha 0) and an opaque
building. References: the first is the block-out to edit; the second the style board; the third
the game's present windmill, for stone, slate and door only (not its sails); the fourth an earlier
body, for stone, slate and window only, drawn from a camera that is wrong: do not copy its angle."

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU GO ON. Write check-body.mjs once in the folder; it reads the
picture and prints each number below with PASS or FAIL. Only the foot is read by machine:
- camera: from tools/building-fit.mjs, roundCamera(png) (ratio, and degrees off), and from
  tools/building-check.mjs, checkFile(path, 't1', 0, { round: true }), which must list no
  problem (background, edge, shadow). Do not run it through the command line form: that writes
  assets/source/buildings-v2/report.json, which is not yours. The foot columns are those whose
  lowest pixel is within 0.4 of the building's width of the lowest pixel of all; W is their span,
  xc their middle, yc = lowest pixel - 0.25 W (the ellipse's centre), s = W / 42.99 picture px per
  game px.
Everything else you read off a zoomed crop of the picture and pass to the script as coordinates.
A picture passes when:
- foot ellipse: minor over major 0.50 +-0.04, at most 2 degrees off (a picture 2 to 3 degrees
  off that passes the rest is a near keeper: kept and reported; if the best attempt is still more
  than 3 degrees off, stop, do not paint again);
- tower: (yc - the topmost opaque pixel) / W within 5% of the block-out's 1.105 (1.05 to 1.16);
  W over the shorter side of the picture is recorded, not a test (Art scales a returned picture uniformly at packing, so the tower's size in the picture does not refuse it);
- stub: two points on the top edge of the stub, one near the cap, one near the plate; the edge
  goes down to the left at slope 0.50 +-0.08;
- hub: the plate's centre, ((x - xc) / s, (y - yc) / s - 4), is within 2 game px of (-16, -34);
- door: its centre column is (x - xc) / W = -0.354 +-0.04; its height over W is 0.251 +-0.03
  (a 2.5 m door reads 0.30);
- clean: pixels with hue 8 to 48 degrees, saturation 0.45 or more and value 0.60 or less (the sail
  timber; the stone's own warmth is not counted) outside the door and the hub plate: at most 25;
  opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0: 0; within 12
  (RGB distance) of selection amber #E8AA48: 0. A picture whose only miss is the near-white count
  is a keeper all the same: record the count, and Art caps those pixels when it packs the sprite;
  name it in a Correction only while another test also failed;
- by eye, on your own look at the zoomed picture: a round smooth tower, no sails or stocks or
  lattice, no plinth, no finial, nothing standing at the foot, nothing orange, the lower-left face
  lighter than the lower-right, the door and the shaft on the lower-left face, the cap slate.

A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole, add under it one paragraph beginning "Correction:"

THE PROMPT, word for word, every attempt: the shared text, then the view's paragraph, then the
closing paragraph (then any Correction paragraphs below it).

Shared text:
"Edit this grey block-out into ONE finished stone tower windmill WITHOUT SAILS, for a pastoral
isometric railway game. Style: the attached style board; crisp clustered shading, broad readable
masses, three or four shades per material. Materials as the third and fourth pictures: cream
limestone courses with a few grey stones, a dark slate cone cap. Light from the upper left, and it
does not turn with the building: the cap's left side and the tower's upper left lightest, the
lower-left face mid-tone, the lower-right face darkest. Nothing brighter than a soft cream: no
pure-white highlights, nothing orange.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera and turn exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees
down. The tower is round and smooth, no facets: its foot is an ellipse half as tall as it is
wide, and every stone course curves like that foot (down in the middle of the face), not as a
straight line. Keep the tower's size, taper and place, and the cone cap's height: the cone's tip
stands where the block-out's does, with a plain tip, no finial, no ball. The windshaft: keep the
block-out's stub, a short round shaft of dark iron-banded timber coming out of the cap and
ending in the block-out's dark iron hub plate, the same oval at the same place and of the same
size; the plate is bare, with nothing on it, and nothing hides its middle."

View r3 paragraph:
"This is the view from the lower right. The stub comes out of the cap's lower-right slope and
points down and to the right along the lower-right face, one pixel down for two across, in front
of the tower, and ends in the plate at the cap's right edge. The dark door is on the LOWER-RIGHT
face where the block-out has it, at ground level on the tower's foot, as tall as the block-out's
door (about a quarter of the tower's foot width): an evergreen plank door with iron straps and a
plain stone frame, no step, no sack, nothing in front. One narrow window may sit above it on the
same centre line."

View r1 paragraph:
"This is the view from behind the windmill. The front is away from the camera: there is no door
and no window on either visible face, only plain stone courses; the lower-left face is lighter than
the lower-right. The windshaft stub stands BEHIND the tower: it comes out of the far side of the
cap, its root hidden by the cap, and points up and to the left, one pixel up for two across, and
ends in the plate above the cap's left shoulder, in the open, not hidden by the cap."

View r2 paragraph:
"This is the view from behind the windmill, turned a quarter further. The front is away from the
camera: there is no door and no window on either visible face, only plain stone courses; the
lower-left face is lighter than the lower-right. The windshaft stub stands BEHIND the tower: it
comes out of the far side of the cap, its root hidden by the cap, and points up and to the right,

Closing paragraph:
"Do not paint the grey slab: the tower stands straight on the ground, its foot plain and clean. No
sails, no stocks, no timber lattice, no square iron plate, no shadow, ground, grass, sacks, people,
text, border or glow. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha 0) and an opaque
building. References: the first is the block-out to edit; the second the style board; the third
the game's present windmill, for stone, slate and door only (not its sails); the fourth the same
tower seen from the front, for stone, slate and colours only: it has a door, a window and a
stub on another side, which do not belong to this view."

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU GO ON. C17c wrote check-body.mjs in this folder; give it the view
(1, 2 or 3) and the table's hub and stub direction for it, and have it print each number below
with PASS or FAIL. Only the foot is read by machine:
- camera: from tools/building-fit.mjs, roundCamera(png) (ratio, and degrees off), and from
  tools/building-check.mjs, checkFile(path, 't1', 0, { round: true }), which must list no
  problem. Do not run it through the command line form: that writes
  assets/source/buildings-v2/report.json, which is not yours. The foot columns are those whose
  lowest pixel is within 0.4 of the building's width of the lowest pixel of all; W is their span,
  xc their middle, yc = lowest pixel - 0.25 W (the ellipse's centre), s = W / 42.99 picture px per
  game px.
Everything else you read off a zoomed crop and pass to the script as coordinates. A picture passes
when:
- foot ellipse: minor over major 0.50 +-0.04, at most 2 degrees off (a picture 2 to 3 degrees
  off that passes the rest is a near keeper: kept and reported; if the best attempt of the view is
  still more than 3 degrees off, stop that view, do not paint it again);
- the tower stands as the guide's: xc within 12 px of 512 and yc within 12 px of 800 (scale these
  by the returned width / 1024), and W recorded against the W in hub.json of r0 (a measurement, not a test: Art scales each returned picture uniformly at packing, by the ratio of the two, so that the four views are one windmill at one scale);
- tower: (yc - the cone's tip) / W within 5% of 1.105 (1.05 to 1.16). The tip is the cone's own
  point, read on the crop: in r1 and r2 the plate stands higher than the tip, so the topmost
  opaque pixel is the plate, not the tip;
- stub: two points on the top edge of the stub, one near the cap or its edge, one near the plate,
  at least 40 px apart; the edge runs at slope 0.50 +-0.08 and in the view's direction (up to the
  left in r1, up to the right in r2, down to the right in r3);
- hub: the plate's centre, ((x - xc) / s, (y - yc) / s - 4), is within 2 game px of the view's
  hub: (-16, -50) in r1, (16, -50) in r2, (16, -34) in r3. The plate must be seen whole, as in the
  guide; in r1 and r2 nothing of the cap covers its middle;
- door: r3 only: its centre column is (x - xc) / W = +0.354 +-0.04; its height over W is 0.251
  +-0.03 (a 2.5 m door reads 0.30). In r1 and r2: no door, no window, nothing dark and
  door-shaped on the face;
- clean: pixels with hue 8 to 48 degrees, saturation 0.45 or more and value 0.60 or less (the sail
  timber; the stone's own warmth is not counted) outside the door, the stub and the hub plate: at
  most 25; opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0: 0;
  within 12 (RGB distance) of selection amber #E8AA48: 0. A picture whose only miss is the
  near-white count is a keeper all the same: record the count, and Art caps those pixels when it
  packs the sprite; name it in a Correction only while another test also failed;
- by eye, on your own look at the zoomed picture: a round smooth tower, no sails or stocks or
  lattice, no plinth, no finial, nothing standing at the foot, nothing orange, the lower-left face
  lighter than the lower-right, the cap slate, and, in r1 and r2, the stub and plate behind the
  tower with nothing in front of the tower where a door would be.

A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole, add under it one paragraph beginning "Correction:"
that names the number it missed, as "your last r1 body's plate stood at (-16, -41) where the
block-out's is (-16, -50)", and keep every earlier Correction paragraph of that view. Always edit
the guide, never an attempt. At most three attempts a view. If none passes, keep the closest in
raw/, copy it to the kept file all the same, mark it kept: false with the test it failed in
prompts.json, and say so in your message: never accept it quietly.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy each passing (or closest) raw file, byte for byte, to
assets/source/windmill-2026-10-17/windmill-a0-r<v>.png and write hub-r<v>.json beside it: {view,
picture, size, box [left, top, right, bottom of the pixels above alpha 128], footCentre [xc, yc],
footWidth W, hub [x, y] of the plate's centre as you read it, hubFromAnchorWorld [dx, dy] in game
px as the hub test computes it, hubTarget (the table's), stubSlope, door [left, top, right,
bottom] (r3; null in r1 and r2), apexTip (the cone tip's y), numbers (every test's value)}. Leave
r0's hub.json and picture alone. Commit "Art: windmill body without sails, r3, r1 and r2 in the
game's camera", push the branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt of each view: the
returned size, the foot ratio and degrees, the tip over W, W against r0's, the stub slope, the hub
offset from the view's hub, for r3 the door's position and height numbers, the three pixel

STATE OF THE FOLDER. ACCEPTED: none. Another window began this job on 19 October (r0 read, no picture painted, nothing committed). WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The old
picture was drawn without the game's camera, so its turned views cannot come from it by any
transform: they are painted afresh on the guides. Three things were learned on the coaling stage and
the Fuel Pump and are built into this prompt: paint on the guide, never on an earlier attempt of
yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one correction at a time, replacing the
last, never a growing pile.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Big Station, a long cream-stone railway station building with a platform
canopy, the SAME building as in the third picture, turned to the view named under View. Style: that
of the second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable
masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The palette
family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B,
slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest,
lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and
nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little. The canopy's posts and their footings stand on the plinth in front of the
front wall, inside it; the wall's foot behind them still runs straight."

dark-green cast-iron posts, the hanging lanterns, two cast-iron benches, the forest-green plank door
and two windows. The lower-left wall is the left-hand side: a quoined stone gable end with scalloped
bargeboards and one arched window, plainer than the right-hand end: no door and no lean-to annex.
The roof is r0's: one dark slate gable roof, its ridge running along the long walls, with the three
chimney stacks (two red pots each) on the ridge at the same places along it; the canopy is a lean-to
on the front only.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the walls named above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. The building is exactly as
large as in the reference: no taller, with no storey more. Its roof is the reference's roof, turned:
the same form and ridge, with what stands on it at the same places."

PART B, r2:

"View. The building is turned half round. The front faces the upper right, away from the camera, and
nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the front's windows or other features
on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the BACK, plainer than the front; the lower-right
wall is the left-hand side. The lower-left wall is the BACK, the same wall as the lower-right wall
of the r1 picture: a plain long stone wall under the slate roof, built as r0 shows the building
built (cream limestone courses, quoined corners, the same base), with a few small windows and one
plain green door, and no canopy, no benches and no lanterns. The lower-right wall is the left-hand
side, the same wall as the lower-left wall of the r3 picture: a quoined stone gable end with
scalloped bargeboards and one arched window, plainer than the right-hand end: no door and no lean-to
annex. Where r1 or r3 is not attached, take that wall from these words. The roof is r0's: one dark
slate gable roof, its ridge running along the long walls, with the three chimney stacks (two red
pots each) on the ridge at the same places along it; the canopy is a lean-to on the front only.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the walls named above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. Do not paint the reference
again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long platform front, with its
canopy, lanterns, benches, door and windows. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the
building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. About the
extra references, the size and the roof. The fourth reference is this same building in view r1 (the
kept picture): its lower-right wall is the BACK, and that wall is the lower-left wall of this
picture. The fifth is this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the
left-hand side, and that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Copy both walls as they stand
in those references: the same height, the same shape under the roof, the same windows and doors. The
building is exactly as large as in the references: no taller, and with no storey more. The roof is
the one all the references show, seen from behind: the same form, its eaves as low as in the
references, and the ridge runs the same way across the picture as in the front view. It carries what
the references show on the roof, at the same places."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/big_station/measure-big_station.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the
  eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view),
  over the base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0 and
  recorded as r0Reference;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, three chimney stacks with two red pots each, the canopy on the front
  wall only, with its lanterns and benches; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line
  names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the block does
  and nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls is plain and straight;
  no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the canopy's posts stand inside the plinth; (d) the
  lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing
  orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/,
which is not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size.
Commit "Art: Big Station turned, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and stop. In
your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height
number against r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly
which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The old
farm picture is the game's Farm Halt today (the review's footprint for it is 1 x 2). Three things
were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are built into this prompt: paint on the
guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one
correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Big Farm Halt, a timber grain barn with a loading dock and a round silo,
the SAME building as in the third picture, turned to the view named under View. Style: that of the
second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses,
three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The palette family of the
whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C,
teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls
mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as
selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little. The dock's platform and ramp and the silo's base stand on the plinth,
inside it; the base is measured on the barn's and the silo's walls at ground level."

PART B, r1:

"View. The building is turned a quarter turn clockwise seen from above. The front faces the upper
left, away from the camera, and nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the
front's windows or other features on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the right-hand
side; the lower-right wall is the BACK, plainer than the front. The lower-left wall is the
right-hand side and is r0's lower-right wall, copied as it stands there: the long timber wall with
the green plank door in its cream stone frame, the window, the timber braces and, at the end, the
round silo with its dark-green pipe and spout on its stone base. The lower-right wall is the BACK: a
plain timber gable end under the slate roof with a small window high in the gable, and no loading
dock, no crates and no ramp. The roof is r0's: dark slate, its ridge along the long walls, the small
louvred dormer on one slope and the cream-stone chimney with its copper pot at the same places; the
silo stands at the end of the long wall where r0 has it and turns with the building.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the walls named above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. Do not paint the reference
again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the short gable end with the loading
dock, the crates and the sacks. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building:
neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The building is
exactly as large as in the reference: no taller, with no storey more. Its roof is the reference's
roof, turned: the same form and ridge, with what stands on it at the same places."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/big_farm/measure-big_farm.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the
  eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view),
  over the base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0 and
  recorded as r0Reference;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, the louvred dormer, the chimney, the round silo with its pipe and spout
  on the long right-hand wall only; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names
  them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the block does and
  nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls is plain and straight; no
  plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the dock and its crates only on the short front; (d) the
  lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing
  orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art:
Big Farm Halt turned, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and stop. In your
message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height number
against r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which
views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The
illustrated town hall has no turned views yet; the game turns it in quarter turns and today shows
the one picture from every side. Three things were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump
and are built into this prompt: paint on the guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a
growing pile.

with cream quoins and a stepped parapet, two columns of arched windows on two storeys and a small
arched attic window.

picture) and the scale k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement. The camera, the plan
(the foot ratio), the height (a proportion of the base width), the light and the same building
decide; and one more test, because a picture whose parts at ground level stand off the footprint is

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Town hall, a two-storey red-brick civic hall with a clock gable and a
bell cupola, the SAME building as in the third picture, turned to the view named under View. Style:
that of the second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad
readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The
palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone
#BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops
lightest, lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0
and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little. The portico's steps and the planters stand on the plinth inside it."

PART B, r1:

"View. The building is turned a quarter turn clockwise seen from above. The front faces the upper
left, away from the camera, and nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the
front's windows or other features on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the right-hand
side; the lower-right wall is the BACK, plainer than the front. The lower-left wall is the
right-hand side and is r0's lower-right wall, copied as it stands there: the brick gable end with
cream quoins and a stepped parapet, two columns of arched windows on two storeys and the small
arched attic window. The lower-right wall is the BACK: a long brick wall as high as the front, with
the same cream quoins and string courses and two storeys of arched windows in the front's rhythm, a
plain green door instead of the portico, and no clock gable, no planters and no lanterns. The roof
is r0's: dark slate, its ridge along the long walls, the two chimney stacks at its two ends, and the
bell cupola with its slate dome and gold finial in the middle of the ridge (it shows, centred, in
every view); the clock gable is on the front only.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the walls named above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. Do not paint the reference
again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long front with the portico, the
green double door, the clock gable, the planters and the windows. In this picture the front faces
away, hidden behind the building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not
show on them. The building is exactly as large as in the reference: no taller, with no storey more.
Its roof is the reference's roof, turned: the same form and ridge, with what stands on it at the
same places."
