PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/town_hall/measure-town_hall.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the
  eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view),
  over the base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0 and
  recorded as r0Reference;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, the bell cupola and gold finial in the middle of the ridge, two chimney
  stacks at the ends; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no
  front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the block does and nothing at ground
  level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls is plain and straight; no plinth, ground,
  shadow or loose object; the clock gable and portico only on the front; (d) the lower-left wall
  lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except
  what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art:
Town hall turned, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and stop. In your message
give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height number against
r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views
passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The
warehouse is the game's goods building and is seen from one side only today. Three things were
learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are built into this prompt: paint on the guide,
never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one
correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

door under it.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Warehouse, a long red-brick railway goods shed with green sliding doors,
the SAME building as in the third picture, turned to the view named under View. Style: that of the
second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses,
three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The palette family of the
whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C,
teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls
mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as
selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little. The canopy's posts and footings stand on the plinth inside it."

PART B, r1:

"View. The building is turned a quarter turn clockwise seen from above. The front faces the upper
left, away from the camera, and nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the
front's windows or other features on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the right-hand
side; the lower-right wall is the BACK, plainer than the front. The lower-left wall is the
right-hand side and is r0's lower-right wall, copied as it stands there: the brick gable end with
cream quoins, scalloped bargeboards and the arched window, the deep corrugated canopy on green
timber posts and stone footings, and the window and green door under it. The lower-right wall is the
BACK: a plain long brick wall under the slate roof with the same cream base course and quoins, a row
of small windows and one plain green door, and no sliding doors and no canopy. The roof is r0's:
dark slate, its ridge along the long walls, the three chimney stacks at the same places along the
ridge.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the walls named above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. Do not paint the reference
again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long front with its three green
sliding doors, two open. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither
visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The building is exactly as
large as in the reference: no taller, with no storey more. Its roof is the reference's roof, turned:
the same form and ridge, with what stands on it at the same places."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/warehouse/measure-warehouse.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the
  eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view),
  over the base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0 and
  recorded as r0Reference;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, three chimney stacks, three green sliding doors on the front only, the
  corrugated canopy on the right-hand end only; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View
  line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the block
  does and nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls is plain and
  straight; no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the open bays show a stone floor and a timber
  frame inside; (d) the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people,
  rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

which is not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size.
Commit "Art: Warehouse turned, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and stop. In
your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height
number against r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly
which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The pump
is a 1 x 1 building the game turns today by showing the one picture from every side. Three things
were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are built into this prompt: paint on the
guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one
correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Water pump, a small stone pump house with a flywheel and a pipe, the
SAME building as in the third picture, turned to the view named under View. Style: that of the
second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses,
three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The palette family of the
whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C,
teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls
mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as
selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little. The flywheel and the pipe stand against the front wall on the plinth,
inside it: the house is smaller than in the reference so that house and machinery fit the plinth
together."

PART B, r1:

"View. The building is turned a quarter turn clockwise seen from above. The front faces the upper
left, away from the camera, and nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the
front's windows or other features on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the right-hand
side; the lower-right wall is the BACK, plainer than the front. The lower-left wall is the
right-hand side and is r0's lower-right wall, copied as it stands there: the gable end: cream
limestone courses and quoins, the forest-green plank door with a small window, the two stone steps,
the hanging iron lamp, the green downpipe, the slate roof with cream coping and the chimney stack
behind. The lower-right wall is the BACK: a plain stone wall: cream limestone courses with one small
window, no flywheel, no pipe and no door. The roof is r0's: dark slate gable roof with cream coping,
its ridge along the lower-left wall of r0, and the cream chimney stack with two copper-red pots on
the ridge, turning with the building.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this

the same form and ridge, with what stands on it at the same places."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/pump/measure-pump.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the
  eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view),
  over the base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0 and
  recorded as r0Reference;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, the flywheel and the pipe with the strainer drum on the front (long
  window) wall only, the door and lamp on the gable; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the
  View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the
  block does and nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls is plain and
  straight; no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; it must still look like r0's pump, not like a
  house; (d) the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or
  smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

assets/source/rotations-535/pump/pump-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with node
tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not
committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art:
Water pump turned, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and stop. In your message
give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height number against
r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views
passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The
grinder is a 1 x 1 works the game turns today by showing the one picture from every side. Three
things were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are built into this prompt: paint on
the guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and
one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Stone grinder, a stone mill house with two millstones and a stone
hopper, the SAME building as in the third picture, turned to the view named under View. Style: that
of the second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable
masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The palette
family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B,
slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest,
lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and
nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little. The millstones and the hopper stand in the front arch and against the
front wall, on the plinth, inside it: the house is smaller than in the reference so that house and
machinery fit the plinth together."

PART B, r1:

"View. The building is turned a quarter turn clockwise seen from above. The front faces the upper
left, away from the camera, and nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the
front's windows or other features on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the right-hand
side; the lower-right wall is the BACK, plainer than the front. The lower-left wall is the
right-hand side and is r0's lower-right wall, copied as it stands there: the gable end in cream
limestone with the red-brick arched window and, in front of it, the small brick-and-stone lean-to
with its slate roof, the forest-green strapped door and the crate of stone. The lower-right wall is
the BACK: a plain cream limestone wall under the slate roof with one small window, no arch, no
millstones and no hopper. The roof is r0's: dark slate, its ridge along the long walls, the
red-brick chimney at the same place; the timber hopper stands on the front only.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the walls named above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. Do not paint the reference
again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long front with the stone arch, the
two millstones and the heaped timber hopper. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the
building: neither visible wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The
building is exactly as large as in the reference: no taller, with no storey more. Its roof is the
reference's roof, turned: the same form and ridge, with what stands on it at the same places."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/grinder/measure-grinder.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the
  eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view),
  over the base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0 and
  recorded as r0Reference;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, the red-brick chimney, the red-brick arched window in the gable, the
  lean-to on the right-hand end only; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names
  them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the block does and
  nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls is plain and straight; no
  plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the millstones and the hopper on the front only; (d) the
  lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing
  orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art:
Stone grinder turned, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and stop. In your
message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height number
against r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which
views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The
refinery is a 1 x 1 works the game turns today by showing the one picture from every side. Three
things were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are built into this prompt: paint on
the guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and
one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Oil refinery, a plant of a white tank, a cream column and a grey furnace
frame joined by copper pipes, the SAME building as in the third picture, turned to the view named
under View. Style: that of the second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered
shading, broad readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong
silhouette. The palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream
#E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left:
roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor
white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little."

PART B, r1:

"View. The camera stays where it is and the plant is turned 1 quarter turn clockwise seen from
above; the plant has no front and no entrance in this view. The plant is r0 turned 90 degrees
clockwise seen from above, about the middle of the plinth, not mirrored: every part stays where it
is in the plant and moves with it. What r0 has along the lower-left side stands along the upper-left
side here: the long white riveted storage tank on its steel cradle (its axis along that side), with
its ladder, railed top and small dome. What r0 has at the far (top) corner stands at the right
corner here: the tall cream distillation column with its yellow-railed platforms and ladder, and the
big copper pipe that loops from its top to the furnace. What r0 has at the right corner stands at
the near (bottom) corner here: the grey steel furnace block on its X-braced frame with the yellow
railing and the dark chimney, and the copper vessels and the white sphere at its foot. What r0 has
at the near (bottom) corner stands at the left corner here: the copper pipe manifold and flanges
that join the tank, the column and the furnace, with the steel frame's feet. The sides of the units
that r0 does not show are built as r0 builds them: the same materials and parts, a plainer finish,
nothing new. Nothing stands outside the plinth at ground level; the column, chimney and pipes rise
above the base as in r0, with the column as tall as in r0.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the parts placed above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. The building is exactly as
large as in the reference: no taller, with no storey more. Its roof is the reference's roof, turned:
the same form and ridge, with what stands on it at the same places."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/refinery/measure-refinery.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the distillation column's height: from the base's near corner row to the column's top,
  over the base's width e.x - w.x (read the column's foot by eye on a crop where r0 and your picture
  hide it behind other units, and say so). Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0
  and recorded as r0Reference;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, the same units (one long white tank, one tall column with three railed
  platforms, one furnace block with a chimney, the copper vessels and the white sphere), the copper
  pipework joining the same units; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names
  them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the block does and
  nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls is plain and straight; no
  plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; no new unit and no unit missing; (d) the lower-left wall
  lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except
  what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to
assets/source/rotations-535/refinery/refinery-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with
node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is
not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art:
Oil refinery turned, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and stop. In your
message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height number
against r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which
views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The
power plant is the game's biggest works building, 3 x 2 tiles in the review (the game data says 1 x
1 today), and is seen from one side only. Its canvas is new: the guides are 1536 x 1024 at 5 px to a
game px, not the depot's 6. Three things were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are
built into this prompt: paint on the guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing
pile.

6ab396d230c15fb51846f79e4c77ce243fdd3c88cf60352c9a1ef9412d158f5d. It is the building as the game

that glow is not in the picture you are to make; paint a transparent background. What r0 shows, to
carry round the building: a long red-brick turbine hall with cream limestone quoins, string courses
and plinth, a dark slate gable roof whose ridge runs along the long front with two long glazed
forest-green roof lanterns (monitors) on it. The FRONT (the long lower-left wall) has three tall
round-arched windows with cream arches and forest-green glazing bars between quoined piers. The
right-hand end (the lower-right wall) is the gable end with a stepped parapet and small finials, a
round window, a big arched window above a forest-green double door with an iron lintel; beside it
stands a low brick annex with a flat roof carrying a forest-green generator casing and black pipes,
with small windows and a green door, and behind the annex a tall square red-brick chimney with cream
bands and a cream cap.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The
grey plinth is the footprint, 804 px wide in the guide (804 px in a 1536 px wide picture), and the base
stands on it and fills 90 per cent of its long side (2.7 of 3 tiles). Art scales a returned picture
UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width,
724 px in the guide (724 px in a 1536 px wide picture). A uniform scale distorts nothing, so the painted
width no longer refuses a picture, and you do not try to hit it: paint the building at the size that
comes naturally, and never resize it yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the
opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer
edge of the right corner, its outline included; chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the
ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned
picture) and the scale k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement. The camera, the plan
(the foot ratio), the height (a proportion of the base width), the light and the same building
decide; and one more test, because a picture whose parts at ground level stand off the footprint is
still wrong after scaling: INSIDE THE FOOTPRINT AFTER SCALING, below. The table gives, per view, the
held width and the plan's foot ratio with its 15 per cent window.

  view  long side     held width (guide px)   held width (returned px)   LL/LR foot
  r1    lower right   724                     724                        0.667 (0.57 to 0.77)

THE ORDER. Paint r1, then r3, then r2. Each of r1 and r3 is painted from the guide of its view and
the r0 master; r2 is painted last, from its guide, the r0 master and the kept r1 and r3 (attach them
only if they passed; if one failed, record it and paint r2 from the master alone and the words).

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Power plant, a long red-brick turbine hall with a tall chimney, the SAME
building as in the third picture, turned to the view named under View. Style: that of the second
picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses, three
or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The palette family of the whole
game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal
#477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls mid,
lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as
selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little."

PART B, r1:

"View. The building is turned a quarter turn clockwise seen from above. The front faces the upper
left, away from the camera, and nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the
front's windows or other features on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the right-hand
side; the lower-right wall is the BACK, plainer than the front. The lower-left wall is the
right-hand side and is r0's lower-right wall, copied as it stands there: the brick gable end with
its stepped parapet and finials, the round window, the big arched window over the forest-green
double door with its iron lintel, and the low brick annex with the generator casing, the black
pipes, the small windows and the green door, with the tall chimney rising behind it. The lower-right
wall is the BACK: a plain long brick wall with the same plinth, quoins and string courses, a row of
smaller plain arched windows and no entrance. The roof is r0's: dark slate, its ridge along the long
walls, with the two long glazed monitor lanterns at the same places along the ridge. The tall
chimney stands at the annex end where r0 has it and turns with the building.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the walls named above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. Do not paint the reference
again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long wall of three tall arched
windows. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither visible wall is
the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The building is exactly as large as in the
reference: no taller, with no storey more. Its roof is the reference's roof, turned: the same form
and ridge, with what stands on it at the same places."

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/power_plant/measure-power_plant.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error from the two slopes alone (checkFile does not know this
footprint); the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above
#ECE4D0 and within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine
can read. Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step,
read the outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (10 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the
  eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view),
  over the base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0 and
  recorded as r0Reference;

- highlights: report the near-white and near-amber counts against r0's; this is NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing): keep them low, but never refuse or stop a picture for them (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, the two glazed lanterns on the ridge, the tall chimney with its cream
  bands, the annex with the generator casing and pipes; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as
  the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the
  block does and nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls is plain and
  straight; no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the big doorway only on the right-hand end;
  (d) the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke;
  nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/,
which is not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size.
Commit "Art: Power plant turned, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and stop. In
your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height
number against r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly
which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The old
picture's camera is wrong and its pad fills about a third of the 2 x 2 plinth, so none of its views
can be reused. Three things were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are built into
this prompt: paint on the guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The
grey plinth is the footprint, 772 px wide in the guide (772 px in a 1536 px wide picture), and the base
stands on it and fills 90 per cent of its long side (1.8 of 2 tiles). Art scales a returned picture
UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width,
694 px in the guide (694 px in a 1536 px wide picture). A uniform scale distorts nothing, so the painted
width no longer refuses a picture, and you do not try to hit it: paint the building at the size that
comes naturally, and never resize it yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the
opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer
edge of the right corner, its outline included; chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the
ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned
picture) and the scale k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement. The camera, the plan
(the foot ratio), the height (a proportion of the base width), the light and the same building
decide; and one more test, because a picture whose parts at ground level stand off the footprint is
still wrong after scaling: INSIDE THE FOOTPRINT AFTER SCALING, below. The table gives, per view, the
held width and the plan's foot ratio with its 15 per cent window.

  view  long side     held width (guide px)   held width (returned px)   LL/LR foot
  r0    equal         694                     694                        1.000 (0.85 to 1.15)
  r1    equal         694                     694                        1.000 (0.85 to 1.15)
  r2    equal         694                     694                        1.000 (0.85 to 1.15)
  r3    equal         694                     694                        1.000 (0.85 to 1.15)

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Battery (the substation), a stone switch house carrying a transformer
and a steel portal frame, the same building as in the third picture in the view named under View.
Style: that of the second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading,
broad readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette.
The palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5,
limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and
tops lightest, lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white
#ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
