THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/battery/measure-battery.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't2x2', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read nothing:
there is no old picture to read for the height; the plan gives the number.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (12 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: foot to the top of the parapet, which is the
  block's 36 game px, 216 guide px, over the base's width: 0.31 (the plan's number, not r0's; there
  is no old r0 to read). Within 15 per cent of that number in every view; r1 to r3 also within 15
  per cent of the kept r0's, read the same way;

- highlights: reported, NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them); the aim is that neither the near-white nor the near-amber count exceeds the old picture's (count it
  the same way) for r0 and the kept r0's for r1 to r3 (Art caps what is left at packing; lamps and
  glowing openings that the old picture has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside the kept r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as the kept r0: the same
  materials, roof form, colours and details, the same transformer tank with its fins, three
  insulator stacks, the portal frame with its crossbeam and the control cabinet with the hazard
  sign, at the same places on the roof (r1 to r3 against the kept r0); (b) turned, not mirrored, and
  the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its
  plinth as the block does and nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls
  is plain and straight; no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the front door on the front wall
  only; (d) the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or
  smoke; nothing orange except what the old picture has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 12 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

assets/source/rotations-535/battery/substation-r<v>.png (r0, r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture
with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/,
which is not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size.
Commit "Art: Battery repainted whole, r0, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and
stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the
height number against r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say
plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

STATE OF THE FOLDER, from the run of 19 October, re-tested on 19 October after the author's width decision (handoff/codex-review-1019.json and codex-review-1019b.json). WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The new
design has no old r0 to turn: r0 is painted first, on the oblong guide, and its kept picture is the
master for the other three. Three things were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are
built into this prompt: paint on the guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing
pile.

THE GUIDES. They are on a branch that is not merged yet (PR #537, art: plan guides for the review-2
repaints), and they are not to be committed to your art branch. From the repository root:

  git fetch origin claude/art-guides-review-2

(its head is 21329dd7 or later; if assets/source/buildings-v2/guides/review2/ is already on develop
in your tree, use those files there and skip the rest of this paragraph). Make a folder OUTSIDE the

THE OLD PICTURE. Art-direction reference only (not a master): assets/source/base-v1/hydro-plant.png,
sha256 9b404cd2224f3728befa6fa7272ee01f06f92c881e384ff21ef2cdb1e422a778. Check its hash first; if it
differs, stop and say so. It is what the game shows today. It is an art-direction reference only. It
has a dark glow round the building: that glow is not in the picture you are to make; paint a
transparent background. What it shows: a stone powerhouse of cream limestone courses with a dark
slate gable roof, three tall round-arched windows with cream arches and green glazing bars, a big
forest-green riveted penstock pipe with copper-banded flanges entering the wall, a sluice gate with
two handwheels and a railing, a forest-green door on a railed stone platform, mossy stone footings.
(Water pump, to be kept apart from: a small square cottage with a chimney stack, a big dark-green
flywheel and an elbow pipe ending in a strainer drum.)

DESIGN, for r0 and carried round the other views. (1) The powerhouse: an oblong hall of cream
limestone courses on a high rusticated stone base, a dark slate gable roof with its ridge along the
long front and a small forest-green vented ridge lantern at its middle, no chimney stack; three tall
round-arched windows with cream arches and forest-green glazing bars in the long front wall and a
forest-green double door at its near end. (2) The water side, on the short end wall at the right: a
small cream-stone intake chamber with a forest-green steel sluice gate and a handwheel on a railed
platform, from which one big riveted forest-green penstock pipe with copper-banded flanges runs down
the end wall into the powerhouse above its base. (3) At the front foot a cream-stone arched tailrace
opening in the rusticated base, a dark arch where the water leaves; paint no water. (4) All of it is
one building on the guide's oblong base: the intake and the pipe stand on the base, inside the
plinth, nothing at ground level outside it. (5) It must not look like the pump: no flywheel, no
elbow pipe with a strainer drum, no small cottage with a chimney stack; its silhouette is the long
hall with the lantern, the three arches, the penstock down the end wall and the sluice with its
handwheel.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The
grey plinth is the footprint, 516 px wide in the guide (632 px in a 1254 px wide picture), and the base
stands on it and fills 90 per cent of its long side (0.9 of 1 tiles). Art scales a returned picture
UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width,
394 px in the guide (482 px in a 1254 px wide picture). A uniform scale distorts nothing, so the painted
width no longer refuses a picture, and you do not try to hit it: paint the building at the size that
comes naturally, and never resize it yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the
opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer
edge of the right corner, its outline included; chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the
ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned
picture) and the scale k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement. The camera, the plan
(the foot ratio), the height (a proportion of the base width), the light and the same building
decide; and one more test, because a picture whose parts at ground level stand off the footprint is
still wrong after scaling: INSIDE THE FOOTPRINT AFTER SCALING, below. The table gives, per view, the
held width and the plan's foot ratio with its 15 per cent window.

  view  long side     held width (guide px)   held width (returned px)   LL/LR foot
  r0    equal         394                     482                        1.439 (1.22 to 1.65)
  r1    equal         394                     482                        0.695 (0.59 to 0.80)
  r2    equal         394                     482                        1.439 (1.22 to 1.65)
  r3    equal         394                     482                        0.695 (0.59 to 0.80)

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Hydro plant, an oblong stone powerhouse with a water intake, a sluice
and a penstock, a new design (the Design paragraph below). Style: that of the second picture, the
style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses, three or four shades
per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The palette family of the whole game: forest
green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82,
copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls mid,
lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as
selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little.

Design, carried round every view. (1) The powerhouse: an oblong hall of cream limestone courses on a
high rusticated stone base, a dark slate gable roof with its ridge along the long front and a small
forest-green vented ridge lantern at its middle, no chimney stack; three tall round-arched windows
with cream arches and forest-green glazing bars in the long front wall and a forest-green double
door at its near end. (2) The water side, on the short end wall at the right: a small cream-stone
intake chamber with a forest-green steel sluice gate and a handwheel on a railed platform, from
which one big riveted forest-green penstock pipe with copper-banded flanges runs down the end wall
into the powerhouse above its base. (3) At the front foot a cream-stone arched tailrace opening in
the rusticated base, a dark arch where the water leaves; paint no water. (4) All of it is one
building on the guide's oblong base: the intake and the pipe stand on the base, inside the plinth,
nothing at ground level outside it. (5) It must not look like the pump: no flywheel, no elbow pipe
with a strainer drum, no small cottage with a chimney stack; its silhouette is the long hall with
the lantern, the three arches, the penstock down the end wall and the sluice with its handwheel."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/hydro_plant/measure-hydro_plant.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read nothing:
there is no old picture to read for the height; the plan gives the number.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the powerhouse wall's height at the near corner s: foot to the eaves, which is the plan's
  block of 40 game px (320 guide px), 0.82 of the base's width of 390.5 px; there is no old r0 to
  read for the new design, so the plan's number is the test. Within 15 per cent of that number in
  every view; r1 to r3 also within 15 per cent of the kept r0's, read the same way;

- highlights: reported, NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them); the aim is that neither the near-white nor the near-amber count exceeds the old picture's (count it
  the same way) for r0 and the kept r0's for r1 to r3 (Art caps what is left at packing; lamps and
  glowing openings that the old picture has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside the kept r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as the kept r0: the same
  materials, roof form, colours and details, the same powerhouse with its three arched windows, the
  vented lantern, the intake chamber with the sluice and the penstock, at the same places (r1 to r3
  against the kept r0); (b) turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no
  front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the block does and nothing at ground
  level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls is plain and straight; no plinth, ground,
  shadow or loose object; set beside the Water pump's r0 the silhouette is clearly different
  (oblong, no flywheel, no chimney stack); (d) the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right
  wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what the old picture has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 12 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to
assets/source/rotations-535/hydro_plant/hydro_plant-r<v>.png (r0, r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept
picture with node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one
size. Commit "Art: Hydro plant, new design, r0, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the
branch and stop. In your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera
error, the height number against r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was
kept; and say plainly which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.
C30a

Colliery repainted on the 2 x 2 plan, r0, r1, r3 and r2 (4 pictures)

Rotations lane. Branch art/535-rotations (exists on origin: twelve jobs pushed to it on 19 October; rebase before you push). Issue #535. ACCEPTED: none (re-tested on 19 October after the author's width decision; numbers per attempt in handoff/codex-review-1019b.json). NEEDED: r0 (all three attempts fail), r1, r3 and r2 (not painted). WHY: r0 attempts 1 to 3: the camera (6.3, 3.6 and 8.4 degrees: both feet flat), the plan (0.716, 0.658 and 0.652 against 0.85 to 1.15), and 34 to 62 guide px outside the plinth after scaling. The three parts are right. The guides are those of PR #537 (claude/art-guides-review-2, not merged): the prompt tells Codex to read them out of that branch into a folder outside its checkout, so they are not committed to its art branch. The plan has an r0 guide, so r0 is repainted: the engine house along the lower-left side, the hopper gantry along the lower-right side, the headgear at the far corner, all inside the plinth (1536 x 1024, 6 px to a game px). Base held at 694 px, feet 345.6 : 345.6. r1, r3 and r2 turn it by the corner map.

Paint four views of the Colliery, r0 repainted too for the isometric railway game in this
repository, for issue #535 (review 2 of the building pictures): r0 first, then r1, r3 and r2. The
old colliery picture, assets/source/base-v1/works-full-colliery.png, is the game's Colliery today,
drawn as a long row of structures at an angle the camera does not have. The plan has an r0 guide, so
r0 is repainted: the same plant, laid out to fill the 2 x 2 plinth (1536 x 1024 canvas, 6 px to a
game px). Then r1, r3 and r2 turn it.

STATE OF THE FOLDER, from the run of 19 October, re-tested on 19 October after the author's width decision (handoff/codex-review-1019.json and codex-review-1019b.json). ACCEPTED: none. r0 was painted three times (attempts 1 to 3). Re-tested without the width gate none passes. Camera: slopes 0.456 and 0.355 (6.3 degrees off), 0.431 and 0.459 (3.6) and 0.453 and 0.299 (8.4) where 0.50 and 0.50: both feet too flat. Plan (window 0.85 to 1.15): foot ratios 0.716, 0.658 and 0.652, the lower-left foot too short. Inside the footprint after scaling (k 0.68, 0.78, 0.86): parts at ground level stand 34 to 62 guide px outside the plinth in all three (in attempt 3 the headgear's footing and the hopper's supports). The engine house, the wooden hopper gantry with the coal bins, the headgear with its wheel and the inclined rope were all there, in the old colliery's materials. Paint r0 again with the headgear on the block's far corner, not past it, and both feet at slope 0.50. Keep that run's pictures and records where they are, go on from the next attempt number (4), and never attach them to a call. A new picture that passes every test is copied over r0.png.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The old
picture is a row of three structures that fills a long strip, not the 2 x 2 plinth; the plan wants
the plant to stand on the plinth. Three things were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump
and are built into this prompt: paint on the guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a
growing pile.

THE OLD PICTURE. Material reference (not a master: r0 is repainted):
assets/source/base-v1/works-full-colliery.png, sha256
7803f567559ca14ddb53a9078580bfe8abe1c7842baedca5de097b1b036e7215. Check its hash first; if it
differs, stop and say so. It is what the game shows today. r0 is repainted in this job (it is the
first call), so this picture is a reference for materials, parts and colours only, never for angle,
size or layout. What it shows: a coal colliery: a tall timber A-frame headgear tower with a grey
iron winding wheel on a railed platform at its top, on stone footings with iron straps, a thin black
cable running from the wheel down to the buildings; a timber loading gantry with three iron coal
hoppers heaped with black coal on X-braced timber legs over a chute and a conveyor; a green-boarded
timber lift-house with a slate roof; and a red-brick engine house with cream-stone quoins and arched
windows, a forest-green arched door and a dark slate gable roof carrying a smaller winding wheel and
a brick chimney.

THE JOB. Lay the same plant out on the 2 x 2 plinth, filling it as the block does (90 per cent of
each side, 691 px wide in the guide): the red-brick engine house stands along the lower-left side
(the front), from the left corner to the near corner, with its forest-green arched door in the
middle of that wall where the guide has its dark door mark; the timber hopper gantry with its three
coal hoppers, the chute and the lift-house stand along the lower-right side, from the near corner to
the right corner; the headgear tower stands at the far (top) corner behind them, its cable running
to the engine house's roof wheel. The groups stand so that the ground-level outline follows the
plinth's 2:1 lines from the left corner through the near corner to the right corner; nothing stands
outside the plinth at ground level. Keep every structure as r0 draws it.

THE SIZE, a measurement and not a test (the author's decision of 19 October 2026, issue #536). The
grey plinth is the footprint, 772 px wide in the guide (772 px in a 1536 px wide picture), and the base
stands on it and fills 90 per cent of its long side (1.8 of 2 tiles). Art scales a returned picture
UNIFORMLY at packing, one factor on both axes and no shear, until its base is the held width,
694 px in the guide (694 px in a 1536 px wide picture). A uniform scale distorts nothing, so the painted
width no longer refuses a picture, and you do not try to hit it: paint the building at the size that
comes naturally, and never resize it yourself. Measure the base as the coaling stage's was: the
opaque width of the drawn base at ground level, from the outer edge of the left corner to the outer
edge of the right corner, its outline included; chimneys, canopies, pipes and the like above the
ground do not count, but anything standing on the ground does. Record that width (px of the returned
picture) and the scale k = held width / measured width in prompts.json, as a measurement. The camera, the plan
(the foot ratio), the height (a proportion of the base width), the light and the same building
decide; and one more test, because a picture whose parts at ground level stand off the footprint is
still wrong after scaling: INSIDE THE FOOTPRINT AFTER SCALING, below. The table gives, per view, the
held width and the plan's foot ratio with its 15 per cent window.

  view  long side     held width (guide px)   held width (returned px)   LL/LR foot

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Colliery, a coal mine with a timber headgear tower, coal hoppers and a
brick engine house, the same building as in the third picture in the view named under View. Style:
that of the second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad
readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The
palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone
#BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops
lightest, lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0
and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/colliery/measure-colliery.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't2x2', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the old
picture once (a zoomed crop) the same way as below, for the height number, and write what you read
to prompts.json as oldReference; for r1 to r3 the kept r0 is read the same way and recorded as
r0Reference.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (12 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the headgear tower's height: from the foot of the tower's nearer legs to the top of its
  winding wheel, over the base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of the old picture's for r0
  (oldReference), and for r1 to r3 within 15 per cent of the kept r0's (r0Reference), read the same
  way;

- highlights: reported, NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them); the aim is that neither the near-white nor the near-amber count exceeds the old picture's for r0 and
  the kept r0's for r1 to r3 (Art caps what is left at packing; lamps and glowing openings that the
  old picture has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside the kept r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as the kept r0: the same
  materials, roof form, colours and details, one headgear tower with its wheel and cable, a gantry
  with three coal hoppers, the lift-house, and the brick engine house with its chimney; (b) turned,
  not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c)
  the base fills its plinth as the block does and nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of
  both visible walls is plain and straight; no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the three
  groups stand at the plinth's corners as the Design paragraph says and none is missing; (d) the
  lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing
  orange except what the old picture has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 12 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The old
picture stands at an angle the camera does not have and does not fill the 2 x 2 plinth. Three things
were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are built into this prompt: paint on the
guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one
correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Ironworks, a brick furnace with a tall chimney, a forge shed and an open
ingot store, the same building as in the third picture in the view named under View. Style: that of
the second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable
masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The palette
family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B,
slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest,
lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and
nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little."

its materials, details and character from it, and its size. Paint the SAME building at the same
size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where it is. It is not a mirror image and not a
copy of that reference: show the parts placed above, and carry the same materials, roof, colours and
details round the building. The building is exactly as large as in the reference: no taller, with no
storey more. Its roof is the reference's roof, turned: the same form and ridge, with what stands on
it at the same places."

of grey iron ingots, the hoist chain with its hook and the plank fence on the cream stone base. What

with its cream base, black iron bands, glowing arched mouth, cast-bed, copper pipe and boiler, and

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/ironworks/measure-ironworks.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't2x2', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read the old
picture once (a zoomed crop) the same way as below, for the height number, and write what you read
to prompts.json as oldReference; for r1 to r3 the kept r0 is read the same way and recorded as
r0Reference.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (12 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the chimney's height: from the foot of the furnace to the top of the chimney, over the
  base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of the old picture's for r0 (oldReference), and for r1
  to r3 within 15 per cent of the kept r0's (r0Reference), read the same way;

- highlights: reported, NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them); the aim is that neither the near-white nor the near-amber count exceeds the old picture's for r0 and
  the kept r0's for r1 to r3 (Art caps what is left at packing; lamps and glowing openings that the
  old picture has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside the kept r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as the kept r0: the same
  materials, roof form, colours and details, the furnace with its chimney and its glowing mouth, the
  forge shed with its teal roof and clerestory, the open store with ingots and the hoist; (b)
  turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and
  r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the block does and nothing at ground level is outside it; the
  foot of both visible walls is plain and straight; no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the
  three groups stand at the plinth's corners as the Design paragraph says and none is missing; (d)
  the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke;
  nothing orange except what the old picture has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 12 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

which is not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size.
Commit "Art: Ironworks repainted on the 2 x 2 plan, r0, r1, r3 and r2", push the branch and stop. In
your message give, for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height
number against r0's, the highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly
which views passed every test and which were kept as the closest.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The
derrick is the game's oil works and is seen from one side only today. Three things were learned on
the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are built into this prompt: paint on the guide, never on an
earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one correction at a
time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the Oil derrick, a rusty steel pumpjack on a skid frame with a tank and a
wellhead, the SAME building as in the third picture, turned to the view named under View. Style:
that of the second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad
readable masses, three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The
palette family of the whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone
#BCA98B, slate #626B6C, teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops
lightest, lower-left walls mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0
and nothing as strong as selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little."

is in the plant and moves with it. What r0 has at the left corner stands at the near (bottom) corner
here: the horizontal grey storage tank with its copper bands and its copper pipe, at the end of the
skid frame. What r0 has at the near (bottom) corner stands at the right corner here: the wellhead,
the copper-banded standpipe on its round flange, with the horse-head of the walking beam above it
and the copper pipes along the frame. What r0 has along the lower-left side stands along the
lower-right side here: the riveted steel gearbox with the crank wheel and pitman arm and the lattice
A-frame carrying the beam's pivot, between the tank and the wellhead, the walking beam running the
length of the frame. The sides of the units that r0 does not show are built as r0 builds them: the
same materials and parts, a plainer finish, nothing new. The beam's pivot, the horse-head and the
crank wheel are as tall as in r0; the machine is the same size.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the parts placed above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. The building is exactly as
large as in the reference: no taller, with no storey more. Its roof is the reference's roof, turned:
the same form and ridge, with what stands on it at the same places."

PART B, r2:

"View. The camera stays where it is and the plant is turned 2 quarter turns clockwise seen from
above; the plant has no front and no entrance in this view. The plant is r0 turned 180 degrees
clockwise seen from above, about the middle of the plinth, not mirrored: every part stays where it
is in the plant and moves with it. What r0 has at the left corner stands at the right corner here:
the horizontal grey storage tank with its copper bands and its copper pipe, at the end of the skid
frame. What r0 has at the near (bottom) corner stands at the far (top) corner here: the wellhead,
the copper-banded standpipe on its round flange, with the horse-head of the walking beam above it
and the copper pipes along the frame. What r0 has along the lower-left side stands along the
upper-right side here: the riveted steel gearbox with the crank wheel and pitman arm and the lattice
A-frame carrying the beam's pivot, between the tank and the wellhead, the walking beam running the
length of the frame. The sides of the units that r0 does not show are built as r0 builds them: the
same materials and parts, a plainer finish, nothing new. The beam's pivot, the horse-head and the
crank wheel are as tall as in r0; the machine is the same size.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the parts placed above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. About the extra references,
the size and the roof. The fourth reference is this same building in view r1 (the kept picture): its
lower-right wall is the BACK, and that wall is the lower-left wall of this picture. The fifth is
this same building in view r3 (the kept picture): its lower-left wall is the left-hand side, and
that wall is the lower-right wall of this picture. Copy both walls as they stand in those
references: the same height, the same shape under the roof, the same windows and doors. The building
is exactly as large as in the references: no taller, and with no storey more. The roof is the one
all the references show, seen from behind: the same form, its eaves as low as in the references, and
the ridge runs the same way across the picture as in the front view. It carries what the references
show on the roof, at the same places."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building
surfaces. Never: rails, trains, people, vehicles, smoke, lettering, signs with text, borders, glow,
or loose objects standing apart from the building."

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/oil_derrick/measure-oil_derrick.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the Samson post's height: from the skid frame's top at the post's foot to the beam's
  pivot, over the base's width e.x - w.x, within 15 per cent of r0's ratio (read it on r0 by eye and
  record it as r0Reference). Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0 and recorded as
  r0Reference;

- highlights: reported, NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them); the aim is that neither the near-white nor the near-amber count exceeds r0's (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, the lattice A-frame, the walking beam with its horse-head, the crank
  wheel and pitman arm, the gearbox, the banded tank, the wellhead, the same copper pipes; (b)
  turned, not mirrored, and the walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and
  r2; (c) the base fills its plinth as the block does and nothing at ground level is outside it; the
  foot of both visible walls is plain and straight; no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the
  rust, the copper bands and the steel look as in r0; no part missing or doubled; (d) the lower-left
  wall lighter than the lower-right wall; no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange
  except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

WHY THIS WAY. The game's camera is 30 degrees down, 2:1. A picture painted from another angle is
repainted, never stretched, squeezed or sheared to fit: that is the author's standing rule (issue
#536), because the fit stretches whatever it is given and a stretched building looks wrong. The
house is the game's most repeated building and is seen from one side only today. The pump-sized plan
fits a 1024 px canvas; the tall canvas is assumed to come back as asked (1024 x 1536); record what
it returns. Three things were learned on the coaling stage and the Fuel Pump and are built into this
prompt: paint on the guide, never on an earlier attempt of yours; the painter's building comes out wider than its plinth (that no longer refuses a picture: the width is measured, recorded and scaled by Art at packing), so what is checked first is the camera, the plan and what stands on the ground; and one correction at a time, replacing the last, never a growing pile.

THE PICTURE PROMPT, word for word, on EVERY attempt, the first included (then at most one Correction
paragraph, below). It has three parts, in this order: PART A, the same for every view; PART B, the
view's own; PART C, the same for every view.

PART A:

"Edit the first picture, a grey block-out, into ONE finished building sprite for a pastoral
isometric railway game: the House, a small cream-stone cottage with a slate roof and a chimney, the
SAME building as in the third picture, turned to the view named under View. Style: that of the
second picture, the style board. Crisp pixel-art-inspired clustered shading, broad readable masses,
three or four shades per material, restrained detail, a strong silhouette. The palette family of the
whole game: forest green #294638, moss #78804C, oat cream #E4D5B5, limestone #BCA98B, slate #626B6C,
teal #477B82, copper #A36B42. Light from the upper left: roofs and tops lightest, lower-left walls
mid, lower-right walls darkest. Nothing at or above cursor white #ECE4D0 and nothing as strong as
selection amber #E8AA48.

Camera: keep the block-out's camera exactly. Orthographic 2:1 isometric, 30 degrees down, ground
edges two pixels across for each pixel down, vertical edges vertical, no perspective. Do not rotate,
tilt or turn the view towards the viewer: the foot of the lower-left wall runs parallel to the
plinth's lower-left edge and the foot of the lower-right wall parallel to its lower-right edge, both
equally steep, neither flatter than the block-out's. Show the whole building with a clear margin all
round: nothing touches the edge of the picture.

Footprint: the grey plinth is the building's footprint and the block on it is its base. The building
stands on the plinth and fills it as the block does (the base is as wide as the block is, and about
as deep); its outer walls rise from the block's edges, and at ground level nothing reaches outside the plinth. The wall feet start at the block's corners, which stand a little inside the plinth's: do not run them out to the plinth's edge. Keep the foot of the two visible walls plain and straight from corner to corner, so that
it shows where the building stands: no bushes, crates, steps, ramps, pipes or loose objects in front
of it. Above the ground the shape is free: roofs, chimneys and machinery may differ from the block
and may overhang a little."

PART B, r1:

"View. The building is turned a quarter turn clockwise seen from above. The front faces the upper
left, away from the camera, and nothing of it is in this picture: no entrance door and none of the
front's windows or other features on either visible wall. The lower-left wall is the right-hand
side; the lower-right wall is the BACK, plainer than the front. The lower-left wall is the
right-hand side and is r0's lower-right wall, copied as it stands there: the gable end: cream
limestone courses, the stepped corbelled gable edge under the slate, and the small window with a
cream sill high in the gable. The lower-right wall is the BACK: a plain long wall in the same
limestone, with two small windows and no door, under the same eaves. The roof is r0's: dark slate
gable roof with ridge tiles, its ridge along the long walls, and the cream limestone chimney stack
with two red pots at the same end of the ridge, turning with the building.

References: the first is the grey block-out to edit, the second the style board. The third is this
building as it is in the game today, seen from the front (view r0): take its materials, details and
character from it, but not its angle, its footprint or any dark glow round it; the block-out sets
those. Paint the SAME building at the same size, turned on a turntable while the camera stays where
it is. It is not a mirror image and not a copy of that reference: show the walls named above, and
carry the same materials, roof, colours and details round the building. Do not paint the reference
again. There, in view r0, the lower-left wall is the front: the long front with the green door and
the two windows. In this picture the front faces away, hidden behind the building: neither visible
wall is the front, and what the front has does not show on them. The building is exactly as large as
in the reference: no taller, with no storey more. Its roof is the reference's roof, turned: the same
form and ridge, with what stands on it at the same places."

PART C:

"Do not paint the plinth itself: no ground, floor slab, pavement, grass or shadow under or around
the building. A real RGBA PNG with a transparent background (alpha zero) and opaque building

THE MEASURING SCRIPT. Write assets/source/rotations-535/house/measure-house.mjs, your own. The shared assets/source/rotations-535/measure.mjs belongs to no job: other windows work on this branch at the same time and rewrote its numbers under each other, so do not use, edit or commit it. Your held width is the guide's number times (the returned picture's width / 1024) for every picture the tool returns, and k = held width / the measured base width (never the width of r0's picture, which is a reading, not a test). checkFile knows depots and one-tile buildings: for any other footprint its complaints about portals or the footprint are not a failure; the camera test is the two foot slopes within 0.03 of 0.50. Your script is built as follows. It reads a picture with pictureOf
(tools/building-check.mjs) and takes the walls' feet with measureBase (tools/building-fit.mjs); it
prints, per picture: the size; the opaque box (alpha above 128); the base corners w, s, e; the
lower-left and lower-right foot (s.x - w.x, e.x - s.x) and their ratio; the base's width e.x - w.x with its outline, and k; the slope of each foot (rise over run; a 30 degree camera gives 0.50) and its
distance from 0.50; the camera's error in degrees from checkFile(file, 't1tall', <view>) of
tools/building-check.mjs (never the command-line form: that writes report.json, which is not yours);
the counts of opaque pixels (alpha above 128) with red, green and blue all at or above #ECE4D0 and
within 12 (RGB distance) of #E8AA48; and PASS or FAIL for each test below that a machine can read.
Where measureBase puts a corner on a crane, a pipe, a canopy post or inside a quoin step, read the
outer corner of the wall's foot by eye on a zoomed crop and record both values.

MEASURE EVERY PICTURE BEFORE YOU RECORD IT, in this order. Before the first call, read r0 once (a
zoomed crop) the same way as below and write what you read to prompts.json as r0Reference: the
height number and the counts of near-white and near-amber pixels.

- width and scale: a measurement, never a refusal. The base's width in the returned picture's px, and
  k = the held width from the table / that width. Record both; the picture goes on to the next tests
  whatever its width;

- plan: lower-left foot over lower-right foot within 15 per cent of the plan's ratio for the view,
  and the long side on the side the plan names;

- camera: both feet's slopes within 0.03 of 0.50 (that is within about 2 degrees of the game's 30),
  and no problem listed by the check. A picture off by more is repainted, never straightened;

- inside the footprint after scaling: take the returned picture in memory (never saved, never
  committed), scale it uniformly by k, and lay it on the guide with the base's near corner s on the
  guide's base corner s (the plan numbers above). The lowest opaque pixel of every column must lie
  inside the plinth: not below its two lower edges (w to s, s to e) and not beyond its left and right
  corners, by more than 2 game px (16 guide px; the outline is 3 px of that). Canopy posts, steps, door leaves, pipes
  and the like standing on the ground count. A picture that fails it is repainted with those parts
  brought inside; never moved, cropped or bent. Record the largest overshoot;

- height, as a proportion (scaling does not change it): the wall's height at the near corner s: from the foot to the top of the wall under the
  eaves (chimneys, ridge, gables and towers are not counted: they stand differently in each view),
  over the base's width e.x - w.x. Within 15 per cent of r0's, measured the same way on r0 and
  recorded as r0Reference;

- highlights: reported, NOT a pass rule (tools/atlas-cap.mjs, issue #465, caps both at packing; never refuse or stop a picture for them); the aim is that neither the near-white nor the near-amber count exceeds r0's (Art caps what is left at
  packing; lamps and glowing openings that r0 has may show, as small as there);

- by eye, on a crop beside r0 and the guide: (a) the same building as r0: the same materials, roof
  form, colours and details, the cream chimney stack with its two red pots, the corbelled gable
  edge, the green door and the two windows on the front only; (b) turned, not mirrored, and the
  walls as the View line names them, with no front showing in r1 and r2; (c) the base fills its
  plinth as the block does and nothing at ground level is outside it; the foot of both visible walls
  is plain and straight; no plinth, ground, shadow or loose object; the cottage stays small and low:
  the tall canvas is not a taller house; (d) the lower-left wall lighter than the lower-right wall;
  no lettering, people, rails or smoke; nothing orange except what r0 has.

CORRECTIONS AND STOPS. A picture that fails: keep the prompt whole and add under it ONE paragraph
beginning "Correction:" that names the number it missed and where it belongs, as "your last r1's feet ran at slopes 0.60 and 0.55 where 0.50 and 0.50 are wanted". It replaces the earlier Correction: no prompt has
more than one. Never attach a picture that failed, never paint on an earlier attempt of yours, and
never resize, crop, move, stretch, squeeze, shear, rotate or bend a returned picture to make it fit:
the angles come from the painting, and the size is Art's to scale at packing. At most three attempts a view, 9 calls in all. If
none passes, keep the closest in raw/, copy it to the view's kept file all the same, mark it kept:
false in prompts.json with the test it failed and its number, and say so in your message: never
accept it quietly. If the camera is more than 3 degrees off in all three attempts of a view, stop the job after that view, commit what you have and say so.

THE KEPT PICTURES. Copy the passing (or closest) raw file of each view, byte for byte, to
assets/source/rotations-535/house/townhouse-r<v>.png (r1, r2, r3). Look at each kept picture with
node tools/building-sheets.mjs --picture <file> (if it refuses the file name as "not a building picture's name", import its exported drawLook and call it for the footprint and view instead, as earlier jobs did; it lays one picture on grass, into .look/, which is
not committed), and at the views side by side: they should be one building of one size. Commit "Art:
House turned, r1, r3 and r2 on the review-2 guides", push the branch and stop. In your message give,
for each attempt: the view, the returned size, the base's width (px of the returned picture), the scale k and the held width, the largest overshoot outside the plinth after scaling, the foot ratio, the slopes and the camera error, the height number against r0's, the
highlight counts against r0's, and which attempt was kept; and say plainly which views passed every
test and which were kept as the closest.

Paint his legs yourself: charcoal-grey work trousers with turned-up cuffs, dark brown leather boots. The grey bar on his right shoulder in IMAGE1 is his axe: paint it as his wood-handled felling axe resting on his right shoulder, held by his right hand where the guide's hand is, the head behind his shoulder; his left arm swings as the guide's does. No other tool. Alpha 0 background and an opaque figure; no ground, floor, shadow, glow, haze, text, border or extra object."
